For a while there, it felt like I had this... this engine running inside me. Sitting quietly was just impossible. I just had to keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. Or hopeless, really. I mean... I haven't noticed feeling down like that. Just... drained from not resting, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Did you notice any changes in your spending habits or engagement in risky behaviors during the time you felt extremely energetic?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't notice anything like that. I haven't been spending more money than usual, and I haven't been doing anything risky. I was just... very busy with my own projects and ideas, like I said. But nothing reckless or out of the ordinary when it comes to my finances or behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I actually do. I... I keep replaying this investment decision in my head. I just keep telling myself that I've ruined my wife's security. So, yes, that weighs on me heavily.\n\nDoctor: How long did this extremely energetic period last?\n\nPatient: It lasted for about five months, I'd say. Right after the investment loss... I just wasn't living like my usual retired self at all. Everything I did during that time just revolved around trying to undo the damage.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you're feeling mostly about the investment decision you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly. Like I was saying a minute ago, I just can't stop replaying that decision in my head. I keep telling myself that I've completely ruined my wife's security. I spent my entire life being the responsible one for us, and... I just feel like I've made a total fool of myself after all those years of doing the right thing. That's what I just can't seem to let go of.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or euphoric during that time when you had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. It's been going on for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the tightness in your stomach and lying awake would still be present if you weren't feeling guilty about the investment decision?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly don't know. I mean, the guilt is just so tied up with everything right now. It's hard to separate it all out. If I didn't feel guilty about the money... I'm not sure if my stomach would still be in knots or if I'd still be staring at the ceiling all night. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that, because the guilt is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stop thinking about the investment decision, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's very hard. For a while there, it felt like I had this... this engine running inside me. Sitting quietly was just impossible. I would be up way before sunrise, calling people, rewriting old proposals, just moving from one task to another without stopping at all. I just had to keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. Or hopeless, really. I mean... I haven't noticed feeling down like that. It's mostly just what I mentioned before—the tightness in my stomach and lying awake in the dark. I don't feel depressed, if that's what you're asking. Just... drained from not resting, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Did you notice any changes in your spending habits or engagement in risky behaviors during the time you felt extremely energetic?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't notice anything like that. I haven't been spending more money than usual, and I haven't been doing anything risky. I was just... very busy with my own projects and ideas, like I said. But nothing reckless or out of the ordinary when it comes to my finances or behavior.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I actually do. I... I keep replaying this investment decision in my head. I just keep telling myself that I've ruined my wife's security. I've spent my entire life being the responsible one, making sure we were set for our later years, and... I feel like I've made a complete fool of myself. It just plays over and over in my mind. So, yes, that weighs on me heavily.\n\nDoctor: How long did this extremely energetic period last?\n\nPatient: It lasted for about five months, I'd say. Right after the investment loss... I just wasn't living like my usual retired self at all. Everything I did during that time just revolved around trying to undo the damage.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you're feeling mostly about the investment decision you mentioned?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly. Like I was saying a minute ago, I just can't stop replaying that decision in my head. I keep telling myself that I've completely ruined my wife's security. I spent my entire life being the responsible one for us, and... I just feel like I've made a total fool of myself after all those years of doing the right thing. That's what I just can't seem to let go of.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or euphoric during that time when you had a lot of energy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. For weeks, I felt... almost brilliantly optimistic, actually. It was like every obstacle in my way was just trivial, like I could easily fix everything. But... well, at the same time, if anyone questioned what I was doing, I just snapped at them. I reacted like they were trying to sabotage me... like I was the only sensible man in the room.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty since your energy levels decreased?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd call it sad or empty. I mean... I haven't really noticed feeling like that, specifically. Like I said, it's mostly just been this heavy guilt about the money and what I've done. But as for just feeling empty or sad... no, I wouldn't say I've felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Did your feelings of guilt and the tightness in your stomach start after your energy levels went back to normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. The energy kind of crashed when all those plans fell apart, and... well, this low feeling, this guilty withdrawal from everything, it started then. It's been going on for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the tightness in your stomach and lying awake would still be present if you weren't feeling guilty about the investment decision?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly don't know. I mean, the guilt is just so tied up with everything right now. It's hard to separate it all out. If I didn't feel guilty about the money... I'm not sure if my stomach would still be in knots or if I'd still be staring at the ceiling all night. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that, because the guilt is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stop thinking about the investment decision, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's very hard. I just... I keep replaying the whole investment decision over and over in my head. I tell myself I've completely ruined my wife's security. It's just always there. I feel like I've made a total fool of myself, especially after a lifetime of always being the responsible one. I just can't seem to turn those thoughts off.\n\nDoctor: Is your guilt over the investment decision affecting your ability to do everyday activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it really is. I mean, I used to handle all our finances, but my wife has had to take all of that over now. She actually stays with me most of the time just to make sure I'm safe, which... well, it's hard to admit. And I used to be very involved in the community—volunteering at the museum, serving on the arts council, seeing friends—but I've just stopped showing up for any of it. I can't bring myself to do it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has your guilt over the investment decision affected your relationship with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's the guilt specifically that's changed things, but... things are definitely different between us. She's had to take over so much, like I said, and she's always checking on me. I just feel like a burden to her now. I don't know if she's angry with me about the money, she hasn't said she is, but... I just feel like I've let her down so completely.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for making the investment decision?\n\nPatient: Yes, constantly. It's exactly like what I was saying a few minutes ago... I just keep replaying the decision in my head. I tell myself over and over that I've completely ruined my wife's security. I spent my entire life being the responsible one, making sure we were set, and now I just look at myself and think about what a fool I've been to throw that all away. So, yes, the self-criticism is pretty relentless.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I reacted like they were trying to sabotage me... like I was the only sensible man in the room.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty since your energy levels decreased?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure I'd call it sad or empty. I mean... I haven't really noticed feeling like that, specifically. Like I said, it's mostly just been this heavy guilt about the money and what I've done. But as for just feeling empty or sad... no, I wouldn't say I've felt that way.\n\nDoctor: Did your feelings of guilt and the tightness in your stomach start after your energy levels went back to normal?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's right. The energy kind of crashed when all those plans fell apart, and... well, this low feeling, this guilty withdrawal from everything, it started then. It's been going on for about nine months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the tightness in your stomach and lying awake would still be present if you weren't feeling guilty about the investment decision?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly don't know. I mean, the guilt is just so tied up with everything right now. It's hard to separate it all out. If I didn't feel guilty about the money... I'm not sure if my stomach would still be in knots or if I'd still be staring at the ceiling all night. I just... I haven't really thought about it like that, because the guilt is always there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to stop thinking about the investment decision, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's very hard. I just... I keep replaying the whole investment decision over and over in my head. I tell myself I've completely ruined my wife's security. It's just always there. I feel like I've made a total fool of myself, especially after a lifetime of always being the responsible one. I just can't seem to turn those thoughts off.\n\nDoctor: Is your guilt over the investment decision affecting your ability to do everyday activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, it really is. I mean, I used to handle all our finances, but my wife has had to take all of that over now. She actually stays with me most of the time just to make sure I'm safe, which... well, it's hard to admit. And I used to be very involved in the community—volunteering at the museum, serving on the arts council, seeing friends—but I've just stopped showing up for any of it. I can't bring myself to do it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Has your guilt over the investment decision affected your relationship with your wife?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know if it's the guilt specifically that's changed things, but... things are definitely different between us. She's had to take over so much, like I said, and she's always checking on me. I just feel like a burden to her now. I don't know if she's angry with me about the money, she hasn't said she is, but... I just feel like I've let her down so completely.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for making the investment decision?\n\nPatient: Yes, constantly. It's exactly like what I was saying a few minutes ago... I just keep replaying the decision in my head. I tell myself over and over that I've completely ruined my wife's security. I spent my entire life being the responsible one, making sure we were set, and now I just look at myself and think about what a fool I've been to throw that all away. So, yes, the self-criticism is pretty relentless.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your guilt over the investment decision would be as intense if you weren't feeling so unwell and struggling with sleep?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... the money is gone, and that's a fact. I suppose if I were sleeping better and had more energy, maybe I'd feel a little more capable of dealing with it, but... the mistake is still there. I'm not sure if feeling better physically would change how terrible I feel about what I did.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
